```text
Page 9 starts with "tion (ΔFPR = 0.000) while preserving detection power, resolving a validation gap: web-overlap labels are near-independent of DIF flags (enrich-ment ≈ 0.96). Applied to six METABENCH benchmarks (27,125 items, 5,227 models), MH-DIF reveals 17.9–31.3% temporal measurement drift (ETS C), driven by item properties rather than accuracy gains; DIF-flagged items concentrate discriminative signal (z = −8σ to −49σ; ρ = −0.597). LLM benchmarks need psychometric monitoring alongside score reporting."


The Conclusion is running over onto page 9, with about 5-6 lines before Limitations begins. I added the IRT θ sentence in §3.2 and promoted that structural table, which together are taking up roughly 7-9 lines of space. To get everything back to 8 pages, I need to trim content from the main text to offset these additions.

Looking at page 8, the right column is particularly tight with §5.2 through §5.4 and the Conclusion all competing for space. The Conclusion itself is already compressed, so I'm looking at whether I can tighten the Discussion sections instead. §5.3 on DIF as a diagnostic lens seems like a place where I could condense the explanation without losing the core insight about the correlation findings. The DIF rate of 31% holds across all six METABENCH benchmarks, with knowledge and reasoning tasks showing higher contamination (≥26.9%) compared to procedural ones (≤18.9%). Simpson's paradox analysis confirms that item properties rather than accuracy improvements are driving the DIF, which doesn't undermine the ranking stability (ρ ≈ 0.997) but does indicate measurement drift between model versions.

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012xV1mrpajWV1yUWj321nof`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\subsection{Implications for Benchmark Temporal Comparability}
5	\label{sec:disc_temporal}
6	
7	The 31\% ETS~C DIF rate extends across all six METABENCH benchmarks (C\% $= 17.9$--$31.3\%$, 45--174$\times$ above baselines; \Cref{tab:cross_benchmark}), with knowledge/reasoning benchmarks more affected (C\% $\geq 26.9\%$) than procedural ones (C\% $\leq 18.9\%$). The Simpson's paradox (\Cref{sec:temporal}) confirms item properties, not accuracy gains, drive DIF. This does not invalidate ranking ($\rho \approx 0.997$) but signals measurement drift across model generations. A fundamental limitation is that MH-DIF cannot distinguish contamination-driven DIF from legitimate capability evolution: the observed 31\% rate represents an upper bound on measurement degradation and a lower bound on genuine construct change. Disentangling the two requires access to training data or controlled ablations rarely available for open-weight models.
8	
9	\subsection{Ground Truth Meta-Critique}
10	\label{sec:disc_groundtruth}
11	
12	The near-independence between DIF flags and web-overlap labels (enrichment $= 0.964$, Jaccard $= 0.206$, $\phi = 0.012$) admits two interpretations: web-overlap labels may be the wrong ground truth for a differential method, or DIF may fail to detect contamination. The semi-synthetic dose-response ($\Delta\mathrm{TPR} = {+}0.508$ at $p = 0.5$) partially resolves this by confirming that MH-DIF \emph{can} detect differential item behavior when present; the near-independence therefore reflects a category error in the ground truth rather than a detection failure.
13	
14	\subsection{DIF as Diagnostic Lens}
15	\label{sec:disc_diagnostic}
16	
17	The GSM1K correlation ($r = -0.478$, partial $r = -0.385$; \Cref{sec:gsm8k}) provides exploratory evidence that DIF captures behavioral change distinct from memorization. DIF-C items are harder ($d = -0.247$) and more discriminating ($d = 0.230$), with a difficulty--direction interaction ($\rho = -0.597$): gains on hard items, erosion on easy ones.
18	
19	\subsection{Recommended DIF Audit Protocol}
20	\label{sec:disc_protocol}
21	
22	These findings motivate a benchmark maintenance protocol triggered whenever $\geq 500$ new models accumulate, using cross-subject matching. We propose graduated thresholds: C\% $< 15\%$ $\to$ monitor; $15\text{--}25\%$ $\to$ flag (label drift-prone and volatile items); $> 25\%$ $\to$ act (rotate or retire flagged items). MMLU's C\% $= 31.3\%$ exceeds the action threshold.
23	
```

</details>

The semi-synthetic dose-response ($\\Delta\\mathrm{TPR} = {+}0.508$ at $p = 0.5$) partially resolves this by confirming that MH-DIF \\emph{can} detect differential item behavior when present; the near-independence therefore reflects a category error in the ground truth rather than a detection failure.\n\n\\subsection{DIF as Diagnostic Lens}\n\\label{sec:disc_diagnostic}\n\nThe GSM1K correlation ($r = -0.478$, partial $r = -0.385$; \\Cref{sec:gsm8k}) provides exploratory evidence that DIF captures behavioral change distinct from memorization. DIF-C items are harder ($d = -0.247$) and more discriminating ($d = 0.230$), with a difficulty--direction interaction ($\\rho = -0.597$): gains on hard items, erosion on easy ones.\n\n\\subsection{Recommended DIF Audit Protocol}\n\\label{sec:disc_protocol}\n\nThese findings motivate a benchmark maintenance protocol triggered whenever $\\geq 500$ new models accumulate, using cross-subject matching. We propose graduated thresholds: C\\% $< 15\\%$ $\\to$ monitor; $15\\text{--}25\\%$ $\\to$ flag (label drift-prone and volatile items); $> 25\\%$ $\\to$ act (rotate or retire flagged items). MMLU's C\\% $= 31.3\\%$ exceeds the action threshold.\n",

          " \\subsection{Implications for Benchmark Temporal Comparability}",
          " \\label{sec:disc_temporal}",
          " ",
          "-The 31\\% ETS~C DIF rate extends across all six METABENCH benchmarks (C\\% $= 17.9$--$31.3\\%$, 45--174$\\times$ above baselines; \\Cref{tab:cross_benchmark}), with knowledge/reasoning benchmarks more affected (C\\% $\\geq 26.9\\%$) than procedural ones (C\\% $\\leq 18.9\\%$). The Simpson's paradox (\\Cref{sec:temporal}) confirms item properties, not accuracy gains, drive DIF. This does not invalidate ranking ($\\rho \\approx 0.997$) but signals measurement drift across model generations. Looking at page 8 and 9:

Page 8 bottom right column shows:
"6 Conclusion
A semi-synthetic validation framework with cross-subject matching achieves zero false positive inflation (ΔFPR = 0.000) while preserving detection power, resolving a validation gap: web-overlap labels are near-independent of DIF flags (enrichment ≈ 0.96). Applied to six METABENCH benchmarks (27,125 items, 5,227 models), MH-DIF reveals 17.9–31.3% temporal measurement drift (ETS C), driven by item properties rather than"


And continuing onto page 9: "accuracy gains; DIF-flagged items concentrate discriminative signal (z = −8σ to −49σ; ρ = −0.597). LLM benchmarks need psychometric monitoring alongside score reporting."

I'm about 2.5 lines over the page limit. I need to trim 2-3 more lines from somewhere. Let me try merging the conclusion's two sentences into one tighter statement that captures the key findings without losing the main points about the validation framework, the temporal drift discovery, and the need for psychometric monitoring. The framework achieves balanced performance ($\Delta\mathrm{TPR} = {+}0.508$) while validating that web-overlap labels remain largely independent of DIF flags (enrichment $\approx 0.96$). Testing across six METABENCH benchmarks with over 27,000 items and 5,000 models, the MH-DIF method uncovers substantial temporal drift in measurement (17.9--31.3% for ETS~C), stemming from shifts in item properties rather than model improvements, with DIF-flagged items showing concentrated discriminative power ($z{=}{-}8\sigma$ to ${-}49\sigma$; $\rho{=}{-}0.597$). This suggests LLM benchmarks require ongoing psychometric oversight alongside standard score reporting.

Now I'm looking at the cross-benchmark validation results across the six METABENCH datasets to see where the temporal drift concentrates most heavily. I'm noticing the task-type pattern here—knowledge-intensive benchmarks like MMLU and WinoGrande show higher temporal drift (26.9–31.3%) compared to procedural ones like GSM8K and HellaSwag (17.9–18.9%), which makes sense given how training data composition shifts affect factual knowledge more than reasoning procedures. I'm trying to tighten up the conclusion by removing the 6-month window result and compressing the cross-benchmark analysis, since those citations are already captured in the table. The key insight is that the semi-synthetic framework resolves the validation gap between web-overlap labels and DIF flags, and I want to lead with that before discussing the broader benchmark patterns. I'm looking at the performance metrics across different benchmarks and datasets to understand how the measurement drift patterns generalize beyond the initial analysis.

65	\midrule
66	Baseline (ext.) & Cross-bench.\ total score & $0.314_{\pm.009}$ & $0.834_{\pm.027}$ & Reference \\
67	Matched-ability & Within-quintile temp.\ DIF & ${\sim}$0.31 & -- & Enrich.\ 0.94--1.04 \\
68	$\theta$-cond. & $\theta$ replaces total score & 0.312 & 0.876 & $r(\theta,\mathrm{total}) {=} 0.9986$ \\

70	\bottomrule
71	\end{tabular}}
72	\end{table}
73	
74	\subsection{Semi-Synthetic Framework Validation}
75	\label{sec:semisynthetic}
76	
77	Cross-subject matching achieves $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0.000$ across all five MMLU subjects (4/5 with $\Delta\mathrm{TPR} > 0.3$ at $p = 0.5$), while within-subject matching inflates FPR by ${+}0.186$ on average. Multi-subject injection ($k \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 20\}$ subjects, 5 random draws each) confirms robustness: $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} < 0.007$ for $k \leq 5$ ($\leq$9\% of domains); at $k = 10$ (18\%), $\Delta\mathrm{FPR}$ rises to 0.026--0.069, marking the boundary of matching variable integrity (\Cref{app:method_details,app:fig_matching}).
78	
79	\subsection{Downstream Impact}
80	\label{sec:downstream}
81	
82	Spearman correlation between full and stable-item rankings is $\rho \approx 0.997$: aggregate ordering is preserved despite 31\% measurement drift. DIF-flagged items nonetheless concentrate discriminative signal ($z = -8\sigma$ to $-49\sigma$ vs.\ random removal), though a circularity ablation confirms DIF-based removal is the \emph{least} disruptive non-random strategy. Model families show distinctive DIF profiles: Llama-3 derivatives gain ${+}53$ median rank positions, Mistral loses $35$ (\Cref{app:fig_downstream}). Removing C-flagged items disproportionately disadvantages 2024 models (cohort gap $= 96.2$ positions, $40\times$ larger than random removal; $p < 0.001$), confirming that DIF items carry concentrated inter-generational signal.
83	
84	\subsection{Cross-Benchmark Validation}
85	\label{sec:gsm8k}
86	
87	Temporal DIF extends across all six METABENCH benchmarks (\Cref{tab:cross_benchmark}). C\% ranges from 17.9\% (HellaSwag) to 31.3\% (MMLU), each 45--174$\times$ above its random-split baseline---none attributable to sampling noise. A task-type dichotomy emerges: knowledge and reasoning benchmarks (MMLU, WinoGrande~\citep{sakaguchi2021winogrande}, TruthfulQA~\citep{lin2022truthfulqa}, ARC~\citep{clark2018arc}) show C\% $= 26.9$--$31.3\%$, while procedural benchmarks (GSM8K, HellaSwag~\citep{zellers2019hellaswag}) show C\% $= 17.9$--$18.9\%$, consistent with knowledge-intensive items being more sensitive to shifts in training data composition across model generations.
88	
89	\begin{table}[t]
90	\centering
91	\caption{Temporal DIF across six METABENCH benchmarks. C\% = ETS~C items ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$) between 2023 and 2024 cohorts. All six show substantial DIF (45--174$\times$ above random baseline), with knowledge/reasoning benchmarks exhibiting higher C\% than procedural ones.}
92	\label{tab:cross_benchmark}
93	\small
94	\begin{tabular}{lrrrl}

108	On GSM8K specifically, semi-synthetic injection with external matching confirms detection power ($\Delta\mathrm{TPR} = {+}0.514$ at $p = 0.5$; \Cref{app:gsm8k_details}). An exploratory GSM1K analysis ($N = 33$) shows a negative correlation between contamination gap and DIF ranking advantage ($r = -0.478$, $p = 0.005$; partial $r = -0.385$, $p = 0.027$), suggesting DIF captures capability change distinct from memorization (\Cref{app:fig_gsm1k}).
109	
110	Even within a 6-month window (2023-H2 vs.\ 2024-H1), DIF remains substantial (MMLU C\% $= 34.35\%$, 181$\times$; TruthfulQA C\% $= 31.33\%$, 51$\times$ above random baselines).

  "new_string": "Temporal DIF extends across all six METABENCH benchmarks (\\Cref{tab:cross_benchmark}). Knowledge/reasoning benchmarks show C\\% $= 26.9$--$31.3\\%$ versus $17.9$--$18.9\\%$ for procedural ones, consistent with knowledge-intensive items being more sensitive to training data composition shifts."
}

  "old_string": "A semi-synthetic validation framework with cross-subject matching achieves zero false positive inflation ($\\Delta\\mathrm{FPR} = 0.000$) while preserving detection power, resolving a validation gap: web-overlap labels are near-independent of DIF flags (enrichment $\\approx 0.96$). Applied to six METABENCH benchmarks (27,125 items, 5,227 models), MH-DIF reveals 17.9--31.3\\% temporal measurement drift (ETS~C), driven by item properties rather than accuracy gains; DIF-flagged items concentrate discriminative signal ($z{=}{-}8\\sigma$ to ${-}49\\sigma$; $\\rho{=}{-}0.597$). }

    "originalFile": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nA semi-synthetic validation framework with cross-subject matching achieves zero false positive inflation ($\\Delta\\mathrm{FPR} = 0.000$) while preserving detection power, resolving a validation gap: web-overlap labels are near-independent of DIF flags (enrichment $\\approx 0.96$). Applied to six METABENCH benchmarks (27,125 items, 5,227 models), MH-DIF reveals 17.9--31.3\\% temporal measurement drift (ETS~C), driven by item properties rather than accuracy gains; DIF-flagged items concentrate discriminative signal ($z{=}{-}8\\sigma$ to ${-}49\\sigma$; $\\rho{=}{-}0.597$). LLM benchmarks need psychometric monitoring alongside score reporting.\n",

Applied to six METABENCH benchmarks (27,125 items, 5,227 models), MH-DIF reveals 17.9--31.3\\% temporal measurement drift (ETS~C), driven by item properties rather than accuracy gains; DIF-flagged items concentrate discriminative signal ($z{=}{-}8\\sigma$ to ${-}49\\sigma$; $\\rho{=}{-}0.597$). LLM benchmarks need psychometric monitoring alongside score reporting.",

Across six METABENCH benchmarks (27,125 items, 5,227 models), MH-DIF reveals 17.9--31.3\\% temporal measurement drift (ETS~C) driven by item properties; DIF-flagged items concentrate discriminative signal ($z{=}{-}8\\sigma$ to ${-}49\\sigma$; $\\rho{=}{-}0.597$). "6 Conclusion
A semi-synthetic framework with cross-subject matching (ΔFPR = 0.000) resolves a validation gap: web-overlap labels are near-independent of DIF flags (enrichment ≈ 0.96). Across six METABENCH benchmarks (27,125 items, 5,227 models), MH-DIF reveals 17.9–31.3% temporal measurement drift (ETS C) driven by item properties; DIF-flagged items concentrate discriminative signal (z = −8σ to −49σ; ρ = −0.597). LLM benchmarks need psychometric monitoring alongside score reporting."


The conclusion section fits entirely on page 8 without spilling over, and page 9 appears to begin with the Limitations section.

  "content": "[Director 指令] [Patch] Round 6 审稿报告 Action Matrix 执行 — P28\n\n目标：EMNLP 2026 via ARR，DDL 5/25。当前 Overall 3.5，目标通过以下修复达到 4.0。\n\n共 10 项修改，按优先级排列。引用不需要验证。\n\n---\n\n## MF-1: Causal Language Sweep (R1-W1, critical)\n\n全文搜索并替换所有暗示因果方向的措辞。已知 3 处：\n\n1. **Conclusion** (`conclusion.tex`): \"driven by item properties\" → \"associated with item properties\"\n2. **Experiments §4.2** (`experiments.tex`, temporal paragraph): \"A Simpson's paradox confirms item properties---not accuracy gains---drive DIF\" → \"A Simpson's paradox confirms that DIF is associated with item properties rather than aggregate accuracy gains\"\n3. **Discussion §5.1** (`discussion.tex`): \"item properties, not accuracy gains, drive DIF\" → \"DIF is associated with item properties rather than accuracy gains\"\n\n另外全文扫描是否还有其他 \"drive/driven/cause/because of\" 等 causal-leaning 措辞，一并替换为 association/correlation 语言。\n\n---\n\n## MF-2: Permutation Null (R7-N1, critical — NEW DATA)\n\n在 `experiments.tex` §4.2 的 random-split 句子之后插入：\n\n\"A permutation test (200 random shuffles of temporal cohort labels, group sizes fixed at $N_{\\mathrm{ref}} = 1{,}080$, $N_{\\mathrm{foc}} = 807$) yields null C\\% $= 0.10\\% \\pm 0.12\\%$ (mean $\\pm$ SD; range $[0.01\\%, 1.14\\%]$; 0/200 permutations exceed observed; $p = 0.005$), confirming that the 31.3\\% rate cannot be attributed to chance label assignment.\"\n\n---\n\n## MF-3: Binary Flipping Ecological Validity (R5-N1, critical)\n\n在 `limitations.tex` 第一个 paragraph (\"Methodological scope\") 末尾或作为新句追加：\n\n\"The semi-synthetic framework uses binary response flipping (incorrect $\\to$ correct) to simulate differential contamination, representing a worst-case uniform exploitation pattern. Real-world contamination involves partial memorization, reasoning shortcuts, and format-specific advantages; detection power under such realistic patterns is untested. The moderate $\\Delta\\mathrm{TPR} = {+}0.508$ at $p = 0.5$ reflects sensitivity to severe differential behavior; the framework's primary methodological contribution---cross-subject matching achieving $\\Delta\\mathrm{FPR} = 0.000$---is a matching strategy property independent of injection realism.\"\n\n---\n\n## SF-1: Delete Section 5.3 (RD2-W2)\n\n删除 `discussion.tex` 中 Section 5.3 \"DIF as Diagnostic Lens\" 整段（3 行）。将 GSM1K correlation 压缩为 §4.7 (`experiments.tex` cross-benchmark section) 末尾的 1 句 exploratory note：\n\n在 \"An exploratory GSM1K analysis...\" 那句（当前在 §4.7 line 108 附近）已经存在。检查删除 §5.3 后 §4.7 的那 1 句是否足够覆盖。如果 §4.7 已有 GSM1K 句子，§5.3 可以直接删除不需要补。\n\n删除后，原 Section 5.4 变为 Section 5.3。更新 label。\n\n---\n\n## SF-2: Expand Section 5.4 → 5.3 Practical Recommendations (RD2-W3)\n\n用删除 §5.3 释放的 ~0.5 page 扩展 audit protocol。当前只有 3 句话的 graduated thresholds。扩展为：\n\n```\n\\subsection{Recommended DIF Audit Protocol}\n\\label{sec:disc_protocol}\n\nThese findings motivate a benchmark maintenance protocol. We propose that benchmark maintainers run temporal MH-DIF analysis whenever $\\geq 500$ new models accumulate on a leaderboard, using cross-subject matching to avoid matching variable contamination.\n\n\\paragraph{Graduated thresholds.} We propose three action levels based on temporal C\\%: (1)~\\emph{Monitor} (C\\% $< 15\\%$): log DIF statistics but take no action; (2)~\\emph{Flag} ($15\\% \\leq$ C\\% $< 25\\%$): label drift-prone items in public metadata and report stable-item rankings alongside full rankings; (3)~\\emph{Act} (C\\% $\\geq 25\\%$): rotate or retire flagged items from the active item pool. MMLU's C\\% $= 31.3\\%$ exceeds the action threshold. These thresholds align with ETS guidelines for operational test maintenance, where C-category rates above 20--25\\% trigger item review~\\citep{clauser1998dif}.\n\n\\paragraph{Integration with existing frameworks.} Temporal DIF rate complements static quality metrics: the Benchmark Health Index~\\citep{zhu2026bhi} assesses item quality at a single snapshot, while DIF tracks \\emph{when} measurement properties shift. We propose temporal C\\% as a fourth BHI dimension (``temporal health''), enabling benchmark maintainers to distinguish between benchmarks that are statically sound but temporally unstable. For anchor-based equating approaches such as Growing Pains FPC~\\citep{habba2026growingpains}, DIF pre-screening can identify candidate anchor items whose measurement properties are stable across cohorts, potentially improving calibration accuracy.\n\n\\paragraph{Tooling.} The analysis requires only a binary response matrix and temporal labels; our open-source implementation processes 12,508 items $\\times$ 5,227 models in under 5 minutes on a single CPU core. We recommend that leaderboard maintainers integrate DIF audits into their model submission pipelines and publish per-item DIF statistics alongside aggregate scores.\n```\n\n---\n\n## SF-3: Claim 2 Framing (R5-W7)\n\n在 `discussion.tex` §5.2 \"Ground Truth Meta-Critique\" 中，调整 framing。当前说 \"web-overlap labels may be the wrong ground truth\"。增强为：\n\n将 \"web-overlap labels may be the wrong ground truth for a differential method, or DIF may fail to detect contamination\" 改为 \"DIF and web-overlap contamination detection measure fundamentally different constructs---global exposure versus differential functioning---making web-overlap labels an inappropriate ground truth for evaluating a differential method\"\n\n---\n\n## SF-4: IRT Fit Note (R6-W1)\n\n在 `limitations.tex` 第三个 paragraph (\"Generalizability\") 之后或第一个 paragraph 中追加 1 句：\n\n\"MH-DIF is non-parametric and does not require IRT model fit; for transparency, we note that the MMLU response matrix shows poor unidimensional 2PL fit (expected for a 57-subject multidimensional benchmark), which does not affect MH validity since stratification relies only on total score ranking.\"\n\n---\n\n## SF-5: Bootstrap CI Asymmetry (R5-W1)\n\n在 `experiments.tex` §4.2 bootstrap CI 之后（\"$B = 1{,}000$ model-level resamples\"）追加：\n\n\"The rightward CI asymmetry reflects a heavy-tailed C\\% distribution across resamples, driven by subjects with C\\% exceeding 40\\%.\"\n\n---\n\n## SF-6: Multi-subject Injection Appendix Table (R7-W1)\n\n在 `appendix.tex` Additional Figures section 中加入一个小 table：\n\n```latex\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Multi-subject injection robustness. $k$ = number of simultaneously contaminated subjects (out of 57); values are mean $\\pm$ SD across 5 random subject draws at exploitation rate $p = 0.5$.}\n\\label{tab:multi_subject_injection}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{rcc}\n\\toprule\n$k$ & $\\Delta\\mathrm{FPR}$ & $\\Delta\\mathrm{TPR}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n1 & $0.000_{\\pm.000}$ & $0.371_{\\pm.048}$ \\\\\n3 & $0.003_{\\pm.002}$ & $0.385_{\\pm.031}$ \\\\\n5 & $0.007_{\\pm.003}$ & $0.392_{\\pm.027}$ \\\\\n10 & $0.047_{\\pm.022}$ & $0.401_{\\pm.024}$ \\\\\n20 & $0.069_{\\pm.018}$ & $0.410_{\\pm.020}$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n```\n\n注意：上面的具体数值是近似值。请从 `experiments.tex` §4.5 现有文本中提取准确数值（ΔFPR < 0.007 for k≤5, 0.026-0.069 for k=10），如果无法确定精确的分列数值，用 §4.5 text 中的 ranges 即可。如果主文已有足够信息，这个表可以简化。\n\n---\n\n## 注意事项\n\n1. 修改后确保 8 页主文限制不被突破（unlimited appendix/refs/limitations）\n2. 不要改动 Abstract（已在 P27 中更新过）\n3. 不要改动 Related Work（已完善）\n4. Section 5.3 删除后重新编号 5.4 → 5.3\n5. 所有修改完成后 commit"

## SF-2: Expand Section 5.4 → 5.3 Practical Recommendations (RD2-W3)

用删除 §5.3 释放的 ~0.5 page 扩展 audit protocol。当前只有 3 句话的 graduated thresholds。扩展为：

```
\subsection{Recommended DIF Audit Protocol}
\label{sec:disc_protocol}

These findings motivate a benchmark maintenance protocol. We propose that benchmark maintainers run temporal MH-DIF analysis whenever $\geq 500$ new models accumulate on a leaderboard, using cross-subject matching to avoid matching variable contamination.

\paragraph{Graduated thresholds.} We propose three action levels based on temporal C\%: (1)~\emph{Monitor} (C\% $< 15\%$): log DIF statistics but take no action; (2)~\emph{Flag} ($15\% \leq$ C\% $< 25\%$): label drift-prone items in public metadata and report stable-item rankings alongside full rankings; (3)~\emph{Act} (C\% $\geq 25\%$): rotate or retire flagged items from the active item pool. MMLU's C\% $= 31.3\%$ exceeds the action threshold. These thresholds align with ETS guidelines for operational test maintenance, where C-category rates above 20--25\% trigger item review~\citep{clauser1998dif}.

\paragraph{Integration with existing frameworks.} Temporal DIF rate complements static quality metrics: the Benchmark Health Index~\citep{zhu2026bhi} assesses item quality at a single snapshot, while DIF tracks \emph{when} measurement properties shift. We propose temporal C\% as a fourth BHI dimension (``temporal health''), enabling benchmark maintainers to distinguish between benchmarks that are statically sound but temporally unstable. For anchor-based equating approaches such as Growing Pains FPC~\citep{habba2026growingpains}, DIF pre-screening can identify candidate anchor items whose measurement properties are stable across cohorts, potentially improving calibration accuracy.

\paragraph{Tooling.} The analysis requires only a binary response matrix and temporal labels; our open-source implementation processes 12,508 items $\times$ 5,227 models in under 5 minutes on a single CPU core. We recommend that leaderboard maintainers integrate DIF audits into their model submission pipelines and publish per-item DIF statistics alongside aggregate scores.
```

```text
1	\section{Method Details}
2	\label{app:method_details}
3	
4	\subsection{Diagnostics}
5	\label{app:diagnostics}
6	
7	Three diagnostic procedures complement the primary DIF analysis.
8	
9	\paragraph{Local independence (Yen's Q3).} The MH procedure assumes that item responses are conditionally independent given $\theta$. Yen's Q3 statistic measures residual correlations between item pairs after conditioning on the latent trait. We compute Q3 for all within-subject and cross-subject item pairs, with $|Q3| > 0.20$ as the threshold for local dependence. Elevated within-subject dependence would indicate that items within the same subject share variance beyond what $\theta$ captures, which could inflate within-subject DIF rates.
10	
11	\paragraph{Domain-specificity.} We test whether temporal DIF concentrates in domains where models improved most. At the ecological level, we compute Spearman $\rho$ between subject-level C\% and subject-level accuracy improvement from 2023 to 2024. At the item level, logistic regression predicts item-level DIF~C classification from domain-level accuracy change, with and without item property controls (difficulty, discrimination). This decomposition quantifies how much of the observed instability is attributable to aggregate domain-level gains versus item-specific behavioral change.
12	
13	\paragraph{Instability decomposition.} When domain accuracy improvement is included alongside item-level controls, we compute the counterfactual C\% that would remain after removing domain-level effects. The gap between observed and counterfactual C\% measures the contribution of domain-level improvement to the total instability.
14	
15	\subsection{Statistical Considerations}
16	\label{app:statistical}
17	
18	\paragraph{Multiple testing.} The ETS~C classification imposes a dual threshold (effect size $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ and significance $p < 0.05$), which provides implicit control over false positives. As a robustness check, we apply Benjamini-Hochberg FDR correction at $q = 0.05$. The random-split sanity check provides an empirical estimate of the false positive rate under the null hypothesis.
19	
20	\paragraph{Threshold sensitivity.} We report results across a range of DIF thresholds ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \in \{1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5\}$) to verify that qualitative conclusions do not depend on the specific ETS~C cutoff. The standard threshold of $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ corresponds to the ETS~C definition used throughout the paper.
21	
22	\paragraph{Architecture heterogeneity.} The METABENCH MMLU population consists of 99.98\% decoder-only models (5,226 of 5,227). This near-complete architectural homogeneity eliminates architecture as a confound for DIF analysis. We verify this by excluding the single encoder-decoder model and confirming identical results.
23	
24	\section{Structural Analysis Details}
25	\label{app:structural}
26	
27	Three progressively refined approaches fail to reduce the temporal false positive rate below 31\%, revealing a structural property of the data rather than a methodological limitation. Matched-ability quintile analysis restricts comparisons to models of similar total score and yields enrichment ratios of 0.94--1.04, with temporal FPR unchanged at approximately 0.31. IRT $\theta$-conditioning with 20 ability strata achieves $r(\theta, \mathrm{total\ score}) = 0.9986$ and FPR $= 0.312$, confirming that the external total score already captures nearly all ability variance relevant to DIF computation. Within-subject DIF analysis on 5 representative subjects, intended to isolate subject-specific effects, finds mean C\% $= 38.1\%$, exceeding the 25\% threshold that would indicate viable bifactor structure. \Cref{tab:structural_comparison} summarizes these results.
28	
29	The temporal FPR reflects genuine item-level instability that persists regardless of how ability is measured or conditioned. Yen's Q3 diagnostic reveals mild local dependence within subjects (11.3\% of within-subject item pairs exceed $|Q3| > 0.20$, vs.\ 2.2\% cross-subject; $5{\times}$ ratio, $p = 2.3 \times 10^{-9}$), which contributes to elevated within-subject DIF rates but is not the primary driver: the cross-subject matching protocol addresses this bias, and temporal C\% persists under external matching (full Q3 distributions in Appendix~Figure~A2).
30	
31	\section{Additional Figures}
32	\label{app:figures}
33	
34	\begin{figure}[h]
35	\centering
36	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_3_within_subject_matching.pdf}
37	\caption{Cross-subject vs.\ within-subject matching for semi-synthetic DIF detection. Cross-subject matching eliminates FPR inflation ($\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0.000$) while preserving detection power ($\Delta\mathrm{TPR} > 0.3$ for 4/5 subjects at $p = 0.5$). Within-subject matching inflates FPR by ${+}0.186$ on average.}
38	\label{app:fig_matching}
39	\end{figure}
40	
41	\begin{figure}[h]
42	\centering
43	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_4_downstream_impact.pdf}
44	\caption{Downstream impact of DIF-based item filtering on model rankings. Despite $\rho \approx 0.997$ overall, individual model families show systematic rank shifts when evaluated on stable items only.}
45	\label{app:fig_downstream}
46	\end{figure}
47	
48	\begin{figure}[h]
49	\centering
50	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_5_gsm1k_dif_correlation.pdf}
51	\caption{GSM1K contamination gap vs.\ DIF ranking advantage ($N = 33$). The negative correlation ($r = -0.478$, $p = 0.005$) indicates that models with larger contamination gaps benefit less from DIF-flagged items.}
52	\label{app:fig_gsm1k}
53	\end{figure}
54

Standard and external matching use different exploitation rates ($p$) because each benchmark's optimal operating point differs; external matching results are compared at $p = 0.5$ for both. Subscript $\pm$ values denote standard deviations across 5 random injection seeds.}

Despite $\\rho \\approx 0.997$ overall, individual model families show systematic rank shifts when evaluated on stable items only.}\n\\label{app:fig_downstream}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{figure}[h]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig_5_gsm1k_dif_correlation.pdf}\n\\caption{GSM1K contamination gap vs.\\ DIF ranking advantage ($N = 33$). The negative correlation ($r = -0.478$, $p = 0.005$) indicates that models with larger contamination gaps benefit less from DIF-flagged items.}\n\\label{app:fig_gsm1k}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\section{Cross-Benchmark Comparison}\n\\label{app:gsm8k_details}\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Cross-benchmark comparison of DIF analysis on MMLU and GSM8K. Both benchmarks exhibit temporal measurement drift with validated semi-synthetic detection power. Standard and external matching use different exploitation rates ($p$) because each benchmark's optimal operating point differs; external matching results are compared at $p = 0.5$ for both. Subscript $\\pm$ values denote standard deviations across 5 random injection seeds.}\n\\label{tab:gsm8k_comparison}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\nMetric & MMLU & GSM8K \\\\\n\\midrule\nItems & 12,508 & 1,319 \\\\\nModels & 5,227 & 6,702 \\\\\nTemporal C\\% & 31.3\\% & 18.9\\% \\\\\nSanity C\\% & 0.18\\% & 0.3\\% \\\\\n$\\Delta$TPR ($p{=}0.7$, standard) & -- & $+0.818_{\\pm.006}$ \\\\\n$\\Delta$TPR ($p{=}0.5$, external) & $+0.508_{\\pm.027}$ & $+0.514_{\\pm.028}$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n",
